The whole provision in English — machine translation, not evidence · claude-haiku-4-5:
Article 17 - Transmission or Transfer of Electronic Data The transmission or transfer of electronic data must be carried out as follows: 1. Obtain authorization from the data owner and ensure that the recipient is able to protect that data; 2. Encrypt data security for important data, such as financial data, banking data, investment data, accounting data; 3. Not falsify the source of the data that is transmitted or transferred; 4. Comply with the contract between the sender and the data recipient; 5. Stop transmission or transfer of data when the data recipient fails to comply. Persons, legal entities or organizations cannot transmit or transfer personal data and state data outside the Lao People's Democratic Republic without obtaining the approval of the data owner or if it is contrary to the law. Article 18 - Access to Electronic Data Persons, legal entities or organizations that intend to access electronic data that is not public data must request permission from the relevant data manager as provided in [text incomplete/unclear].

Law on the Protection of Electronic Information, Art. 18 PROVISION: Law on the Protection of Electronic Information | Art. 18 | p.7 :: Unless otherwise provided, A natural person, juristic person or organization shall not transmit or transfer personal data and state information outside the Lao PDR if it has not received the consent of the data owner or if it violates the law. Article 18 Access to Electronic Information A natural person, juristic person or organization that intends to access electronic information that is not public information must submit to the relevant data controller as defined in Article 16 of this law for consideration in accordance with the law. Article 19 Update or Correction of Electronic Information A data owner may request the data controller to update or correct electronic information of the owner or request to stop transmitting or transferring the owner's information to a third person.

Traps true: provision_in_force, sectoral_scope

The whole provision in English — machine translation, not evidence · claude-haiku-4-5:
Law on Payment Systems (Amended Version), Art. 19 SECTION ON DEVELOPMENT OF PAYMENT SYSTEMS AND PAYMENT TECHNOLOGY Legal persons and organizations having the purpose of developing payment technology in the Lao PDR must request permission from the central bank of the Lao PDR as prescribed in the regulations. Article 19 (amended) Cross-Border Payments Cross-border payment means the interconnected use of payment systems in the Lao PDR with foreign countries to support the provision of payment services between payers or payees who have residence in the Lao PDR and payers or payees who have residence in foreign countries. Cross-border payment may be carried out in the form of direct payment systems and payment service provision systems with bilateral or multilateral forms according to the permission of the central bank of the Lao PDR. Have a source of foreign currency funds that is sufficient; 4. Have recognition or confirmation of connection to a cross-border payment service system from the relevant competent authorities in foreign countries, and ensure that transaction data is stored in the Lao PDR; 5. Have completed resolution of all outstanding issues from audits and received confirmation of the resolution thereof from the relevant sector within sixty days prior to the date of authorization application; 6. Have a clear business operation plan including mechanisms for reconciliation and account reconciliation; 7. Have an internal control system and a good risk management system; 8. Law on Cybersecurity, Art. 12 PROVISION: Law on Cybersecurity | Art. 12 | Part II, p.7 :: Including planning for continuous development, data backup, disaster recovery, or maintenance and protection of systems and management of capacity to provide support to individuals or authorized systems. Article 12 Authentication and Access Control Authentication is the process of verifying the validity of the link between a person, legal entity or organization and information and characteristics derived from the database of the related organization which are cited as belonging to that person, legal entity or organization. Access control is a system which ensures that only authorized persons or systems can access information or technological resources based on rights and necessity for access with systematic access verification. Article 13 Digital Evidence and Responsibility Digital evidence is information recorded by electronic equipment and stored in a system or during transmission and receipt, which can prove an action or any event that occurred.

The whole provision in English — machine translation, not evidence · claude-haiku-4-5:
Law on Cybersecurity, Article 3 PROVISION: Law on Cybersecurity | Article 3 | p.3 :: Protection of communication technology systems, information and news from cyber attacks or damage from cyber threats, including maintaining the integrity of systems, networks, and ensuring the availability and operability of such systems. Article 3 Definition of Terms Terms used in this Law shall have the following meanings: 1. Cyber refers to communication technology systems for information and news that comprise computer networks, information and news technology infrastructure, control systems, connection equipment, and all online activities; 2. Critical National Information Infrastructure Organizations refer to legal persons, organizations, or agencies that own, operate, or administer critical systems important to the nation, the cessation or destruction of which would have serious consequences for the security, economic and social conditions of the Lao PDR; 3. Computer Emergency Response Unit refers to a team of experts responsible for managing and preventing cybersecurity incidents; 4. Cyber Threat refers to danger or risk that may occur to systems, networks, or information in cyberspace, such as cyber attacks, malware, or unauthorized access to personal data; 5. Cyberspace refers to the digital environment that comprises systems, networks, electronic information, and interaction between all users connected through communication technology; 6. Cyber Attack refers to any intentional act that obstructs or accesses without authorization systems, networks, or information, such as denial of service attacks, system intrusion, or computer system vulnerabilities; 7. Firewall refers to a network security system that monitors and controls data traffic in and out, prevents unauthorized access, and protects against internet threats; 8. Denial of Service Attack refers to a cyber attack that uses control of multiple devices to request large amounts of data to a target system, causing that system to fail or [text becomes unclear/corrupted]

Law on Cybersecurity | Art. 31 PROVISION: Law on Cybersecurity | Art. 31 | Part [unclear reference], p.11 :: Information and prioritization of threats through clear reporting and warning procedures, together with ensuring twenty-four-hour operations with a team of expert monitors and having contingency plans in case of emergency incidents. LAW ON CYBERSECURITY, Art. 4 PROVISION: Law on Cybersecurity | Art. 4 | p.5 :: d. Individuals, legal entities or organizations outside [the country] that provide services in the field of information technology, information services, cloud services, maintenance of systems, or other cybersecurity services that may access or process data of [government] agencies. Article 4 State Policy on Cybersecurity Work The State regards cybersecurity as a priority in the work of national defense-peace maintenance, economic-social development, science, technology and foreign affairs. The State considers cybersecurity work important and supports it through budget allocation, provision of necessary materials and equipment, infrastructure development, development, maintenance and mobilization of human resources that are professionals and experts in using modern technology in order to make such work effective and efficient. The State promotes individuals, legal entities and organizations both domestic and foreign to cooperate and participate in preventing cybersecurity threats that are harmful to the national infrastructure, information and communications that are important to the nation and the orderliness of society. The State encourages and promotes individuals, legal entities and organizations to conduct research, investment, technology development, products and services to prevent cybersecurity threats. The whole provision in English — machine translation, not evidence · claude-haiku-4-5:
Law on Cybersecurity, Art. 9 PROVISION: Law on Cybersecurity | Art. 9 | Part II, p.6 :: Cybersecurity Essential Components of Cybersecurity are as follows: - Confidentiality; - Integrity; - Availability; - Authentication and Access Control; - Digital Evidence and Accountability. Article 42: System Security Standards The provision of electronic signature certification services must have a security protection system installed with special properties that does not cause electrical and electronic equipment to malfunction and has been verified to guarantee security protection against fire. Article 42: System Security Standards Security standards for electronic signature certification services include: 1. Must ensure the security of all user service data so that it is not destroyed, altered, or stolen; 2. Must have a data backup system to ensure normal use; 3. Must ensure the security of the system so that it does not become a loophole that malicious persons use to attack, conduct theft, or cause harm to others; 4. Must have a warning system and prevention of attacks and unauthorized connections through the computer system; 5. Devices used for recording or storing data if they malfunction or are no longer used must be destroyed by appropriate methods to ensure they cannot be reused or the data recovered again; 6. Important data backups must be stored outside the location of the system to prevent data loss in case of emergency; 7. All technical systems used in providing the service must be located in the Lao PDR; 8. Technical standards as defined by the Ministry of Posts, Telecommunications and Communications. Technical Standards Specific Article 43: Generation and Installation of Key Pair The provider of electronic signature certification services must create and install a key pair for electronic signature certificates.

AGREEMENT ON MEASURES TO REFORM OFFENDERS REGARDING COMPUTER SYSTEM CRIME, Art. 3 PROVISION: Agreement on Measures to Reform Offenders Regarding Computer System Crime | Art. 3 | p.1 :: Offender regarding computer system crime means the determination of the rate of administrative fine against a person, legal person or organization that has committed offenses under Article 60 of the Law on Prevention and Suppression of Computer System Crime. Article 8 Explanation of Terms The terms used in this Agreement have the following meanings: 1. Crime refers to the offense as defined in the Penal Code and other laws that impose criminal penalties; 2. Computer system means an electronic device or set of computer equipment connected to an operating system through the determination of commands, sets of commands or other connections so that the electronic device or set of computer equipment can perform automatic data processing in one computer unit or multiple units such as: personal computers, server systems, telecommunication devices connected to one another through computer networks and internet networks; 3. Computer data means data, information, programs or databases, personal data, transaction data through computer systems in a form that can be processed and that allows the computer system to function; 4. Personal data means data that is related to or indicates characteristics or the identity, movements of a person, legal person or organization directly or indirectly; 5. Server system means a system providing services through a computer system, consisting of server database, web server, mail server, file server. Article 4 Scope of Application This Agreement applies to persons, legal persons and organizations that manage information and electronic devices in the Lao People's Democratic Republic.

ARTICLE 26 RECORDING AND REPORTING OF OCCUPATIONAL ACCIDENTS AND OCCUPATIONAL DISEASES The employer must investigate and record the causes of occupational accidents and occupational diseases. Records of the causes of occupational accidents must be kept for at least 5 years and at least 20 years for occupational diseases. The employer must report the number of workers affected by occupational accidents and occupational diseases to the Labour Management Authority every three months, six months and one year. In case of a serious occupational accident at the workplace or where a large number of workers have suffered harm from work, the employer must do the following: 1. If there is death, serious injury or damage to the workplace requiring cessation of operations, the employer must notify the Labour Management Authority immediately and submit a written report concerning the causes that occurred, including the damage, corrective measures and prevention methods within twenty-eight hours; 2. The employer must notify the Social Security Authority about the number of workers injured and killed from occupational accidents within seven days in order to receive allowances according to the law on social security, and also report to the Labour Management Authority; 3. If the employer does not report according to points 1 and 2 above, the worker or a representative of the worker or a labour committee or a family member of the injured person also has the right to report. The whole provision in English — machine translation, not evidence · claude-haiku-4-5:
Article 18 - Consideration of Designation to the Domestic List The Ministry of Public Security shall consider proposals for designation to the domestic list on the basis of the following grounds: 1. A natural person, legal person, organization or group of persons, if there is substantive and sufficient evidence that can prove or confirm that such natural person, legal person, organization or group of persons has the purpose of providing assistance, support, funding, equipment or technology, goods or services, or conduct or activities as defined in the Law on Counter-Terrorism, Anti-Money Laundering and Countering the Financing of Terrorism and related laws and regulations. 2. A natural person, legal person, organization or group of persons that a foreign state requests to be designated to the domestic list, whereby the requesting state has substantive and sufficient evidence that can confirm that such person has conduct or activities as defined in the Law on Counter-Terrorism, Anti-Money Laundering and Countering the Financing of Terrorism and related laws and regulations. Following consideration by the Ministry of Public Security and finding that there is substantive and sufficient evidence that can confirm that the conduct or activities of a natural person, legal person, organization or group of persons meets the grounds as specified in the clauses of this Article, it shall propose to the competent authority to consider designation to the domestic list or submit a request to foreign states immediately through the Ministry of Foreign Affairs without necessity of going through criminal proceedings or having a court judgment and without necessity of a criminal act having occurred. The Ministry of Public Security shall inform the rights, obligations and details of the request to the natural person, legal person, organization or group of persons that is designated to the domestic list after the competent authority has designated the list.

The whole provision in English — machine translation, not evidence · claude-haiku-4-5:
Article 20: Responsibilities of Public Security Authorities in the Management of Information through the Internet Public security authorities shall be responsible for: 1. Receiving notifications from persons, legal entities and organizations in cases of criminal offences related to national security and public order; 2. Collecting, verifying and investigating information through the Internet that has the nature of threatening national security, state secrets and public order; 3. Investigating and prosecuting persons, legal entities and organizations that violate this law. Article 21: Responsibilities of Education and Sports Authorities in the Management of Information through the Internet Education and Sports Authorities shall be responsible for promulgating regulations regarding the management of Internet use [text becomes unclear/incomplete in source]
